I'll start tapping the tiles on the table, just in the same pattern, over and over. And sometimes I'll hold my hand up, kind of like a policeman stopping traffic. The other fellas, they get a bit frustrated with me. They just tell me to stop it and play my turn, but... I just keep doing it. I'm not really sure why.\n\nDoctor: Did these messages and voices start all of a sudden?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say it was all of a sudden, no. It's been... well, it's been going on for a bit. The voices and those special messages, they've been happening on and off for about nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you started any new medicines or had any alcohol or drug use around the time these voices and messages began?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't started any new medicines. I'm just taking the same ones I always have. And I don't drink alcohol or use any drugs... never really been one for that, to be honest. So, no, nothing like that changed when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose that's true. I mean, I still love my family and all, but... well, a lot of days I just sit at the kitchen table. I don't really say much of anything. I used to keep up with my garden, and I'd go to choir practice, but lately I just let them go by. I just sit there without really feeling like moving or doing any of it.\n\nDoctor: How old are you?\n\nPatient: I'm 84 years old.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I feel depressed, really. I'm not sad or crying or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just don't have the energy or the interest to do much of anything anymore. But I wouldn't call it feeling down in the dumps most days, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if they're trying to harm me exactly, but... I do know I need to be careful. I came in today because the men at the domino table, they say things. And sometimes the radio announcer does it too. They give these little messages that are just meant for me. My wife tells me it's just regular talk, but I know better. I know they're warning me about something, telling me to watch my step.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble with your hearing lately?\n\nPatient: My hearing? The other fellas, they get a bit frustrated with me. They just tell me to stop it and play my turn, but... I just keep doing it. I'm not really sure why.\n\nDoctor: Did these messages and voices start all of a sudden?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say it was all of a sudden, no. It's been... well, it's been going on for a bit. The voices and those special messages, they've been happening on and off for about nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you started any new medicines or had any alcohol or drug use around the time these voices and messages began?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't started any new medicines. I'm just taking the same ones I always have. And I don't drink alcohol or use any drugs... never really been one for that, to be honest. So, no, nothing like that changed when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose that's true. I mean, I still love my family and all, but... well, a lot of days I just sit at the kitchen table. I don't really say much of anything. I used to keep up with my garden, and I'd go to choir practice, but lately I just let them go by. I just sit there without really feeling like moving or doing any of it.\n\nDoctor: How old are you?\n\nPatient: I'm 84 years old.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I feel depressed, really. I'm not sad or crying or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just don't have the energy or the interest to do much of anything anymore. But I wouldn't call it feeling down in the dumps most days, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if they're trying to harm me exactly, but... I do know I need to be careful. I came in today because the men at the domino table, they say things. And sometimes the radio announcer does it too. They give these little messages that are just meant for me. My wife tells me it's just regular talk, but I know better. I know they're warning me about something, telling me to watch my step.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble with your hearing lately?\n\nPatient: My hearing? Well, my ears work alright for an old man, I suppose. But... sometimes, mostly in the afternoons when the house gets real quiet, I do hear things. I hear my old friend call my name from out on the porch. Or sometimes I'll hear him humming a choir song. It sounds clear as a bell, just exactly like he is standing right there next to me. So I hear that just fine, even if nobody else is around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thinking suddenly gets much more confused than usual?\n\nPatient: Confused? Well... no, I wouldn't say so. I mean, I might forget where I set my glasses down sometimes, just being an old man and all, but my thoughts don't suddenly get all jumbled up on me. My mind stays pretty clear, near as I can tell.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. when the house is quiet, I hear my old friend call my name from the porch or hum a choir song, clear like he is standing right there.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: At domino games I have started tapping the tiles in the same pattern over and over and holding my hand up like I am stopping traffic, even though the others tell me to just play.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I still love my family, but many days I just sit at the kitchen table, say very little, and let the garden and choir practice go by without feeling like moving.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: There are weeks when I wake up feeling so bright and important that I laugh loud and talk fast, but if someone slows me down or questions me, I get sharp with them.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: When that energy comes, I start cleaning the yard, fixing a chair, writing plans for church, and moving things around the house all in the same morning.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I get sure that I am the one who can bring the whole neighborhood together again, like I have a special gift for making people listen and follow my plans.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: On those nights I may sleep only two or three hours, then get up before daylight feeling ready to work like I had a full rest.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My mind runs from the choir to baseball to repairs to my friend and back again so fast that I cannot hold one thought long enough to explain it right.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: If I am trying to read the church paper or talk with my wife, a car outside, a bird, or a sound from the radio pulls me away and I forget what I was doing.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During my bright, restless weeks when I am sleeping little and making big plans, I still hear my friend’s voice and feel the radio or domino men are sending me warnings.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: Since all this started, more months have been taken up by those high-energy spells and their aftereffects than by my calmer stretches.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a quiet month when I was sleeping normally and not full of big plans, but I still heard my friend on the porch and Well, my ears work alright for an old man, I suppose. But... sometimes, mostly in the afternoons when the house gets real quiet, I do hear things. I hear my old friend call my name from out on the porch. Or sometimes I'll hear him humming a choir song. It sounds clear as a bell, just exactly like he is standing right there next to me. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22, F06.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I came in because... well, it's about the men I play dominoes with, and sometimes the man on the radio. They've been giving me these little messages. Just small things, but they're meant for me. My wife, she tells me it's just regular talk and I shouldn't pay it any mind, but... I know what I hear. I know they're warning me to be careful. I just thought maybe I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear a voice speaking to you when no one is actually there?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I do. Some afternoons, when the house gets real quiet... I hear my old friend. He calls my name from out on the porch, or sometimes I hear him humming a choir song. It sounds clear as day, just like he's standing right there next to me, but... there's nobody around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any brain or nerve condition, like dementia, Parkinson’s disease, seizures, or a stroke?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I haven't. Never had a stroke or... or seizures, nothing like that. And my mind's still pretty sharp, so no dementia or Parkinson's either. I haven't ever been told I have any kind of brain condition like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your thoughts or behavior felt badly jumbled up, or other people said you were acting very confused or disorganized?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know about being confused, but... when I'm playing dominoes lately, I've caught myself doing this thing. I'll start tapping the tiles on the table, just in the same pattern, over and over. And sometimes I'll hold my hand up, kind of like a policeman stopping traffic. The other fellas, they get a bit frustrated with me. They just tell me to stop it and play my turn, but... I just keep doing it. I'm not really sure why.\n\nDoctor: Did these messages and voices start all of a sudden?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say it was all of a sudden, no. It's been... well, it's been going on for a bit. The voices and those special messages, they've been happening on and off for about nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you started any new medicines or had any alcohol or drug use around the time these voices and messages began?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't started any new medicines. I'm just taking the same ones I always have. And I don't drink alcohol or use any drugs... never really been one for that, to be honest. So, no, nothing like that changed when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose that's true. I mean, I still love my family and all, but... well, a lot of days I just sit at the kitchen table. I don't really say much of anything. I used to keep up with my garden, and I'd go to choir practice, but lately I just let them go by. I just sit there without really feeling like moving or doing any of it.\n\nDoctor: How old are you?\n\nPatient: I'm 84 years old.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I feel depressed, really. I'm not sad or crying or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just don't have the energy or the interest to do much of anything anymore. But I wouldn't call it feeling down in the dumps most days, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if they're trying to harm me exactly, but... I do know I need to be careful. I came in today because the men at the domino table, they say things. And sometimes the radio announcer does it too. They give these little messages that are just meant for me. My wife tells me it's just regular talk, but I know better. I know they're warning me about something, telling me to watch my step.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble with your hearing lately?\n\nPatient: My hearing? Well, my ears work alright for an old man, I suppose. But... sometimes, mostly in the afternoons when the house gets real quiet, I do hear things. I hear my old friend call my name from out on the porch. Or sometimes I'll hear him humming a choir song. It sounds clear as a bell, just exactly like he is standing right there next to me. So I hear that just fine, even if nobody else is around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thinking suddenly gets much more confused than usual?\n\nPatient: Confused? Well... no, I wouldn't say so. I mean, I might forget where I set my glasses down sometimes, just being an old man and all, but my thoughts don't suddenly get all jumbled up on me. My mind stays pretty clear, near as I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any serious medical illness recently?\n\nPatient: Serious illness? No, I wouldn't say so. I mean... I get my regular aches and pains, just from getting old, but I haven't been sick. No hospital trips or anything like that lately. My body is holding up pretty well, all things considered.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that someone was putting thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: Putting thoughts into my mind? No... no, I can't say I've ever felt anything like that. My thoughts are just my own. I mean, nobody else is up there putting things in my head, if that's what you're asking. It's just my own regular thinking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been seeing things that other people cannot see?\n\nPatient: Seeing things? No, I can't say I see anything that isn't there. But... well, I do hear things sometimes. Some afternoons, when the house gets real quiet, I hear my old friend. He'll call my name from out on the porch, or sometimes I hear him humming an old choir song. It sounds clear as day, just exactly like he's standing right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you use a hearing aid?\n\nPatient: A hearing aid? No, no I don't. My hearing is... well, it's pretty good for my age, I think. I haven't ever needed one of those. I can hear the TV just fine, and I can hear people talking to me clear enough. So, no, I don't use anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by a doctor that you have Alzheimer’s disease or any other kind of dementia?\n\nPatient: Alzheimer's? No, no doctor has ever told me that. I mean, my memory ain't perfect—I am 84 years old, after all—but... no, nobody's ever said I have dementia or anything like that. My mind is still doing alright, near as I can tell.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. So, no, nothing like that changed when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose that's true. I mean, I still love my family and all, but... well, a lot of days I just sit at the kitchen table. I don't really say much of anything. I used to keep up with my garden, and I'd go to choir practice, but lately I just let them go by. I just sit there without really feeling like moving or doing any of it.\n\nDoctor: How old are you?\n\nPatient: I'm 84 years old.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I feel depressed, really. I'm not sad or crying or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just don't have the energy or the interest to do much of anything anymore. But I wouldn't call it feeling down in the dumps most days, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if they're trying to harm me exactly, but... I do know I need to be careful. I came in today because the men at the domino table, they say things. And sometimes the radio announcer does it too. They give these little messages that are just meant for me. My wife tells me it's just regular talk, but I know better. I know they're warning me about something, telling me to watch my step.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble with your hearing lately?\n\nPatient: My hearing? Well, my ears work alright for an old man, I suppose. But... sometimes, mostly in the afternoons when the house gets real quiet, I do hear things. I hear my old friend call my name from out on the porch. Or sometimes I'll hear him humming a choir song. It sounds clear as a bell, just exactly like he is standing right there next to me. So I hear that just fine, even if nobody else is around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thinking suddenly gets much more confused than usual?\n\nPatient: Confused? Well... no, I wouldn't say so. I mean, I might forget where I set my glasses down sometimes, just being an old man and all, but my thoughts don't suddenly get all jumbled up on me. My mind stays pretty clear, near as I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any serious medical illness recently?\n\nPatient: Serious illness? No, I wouldn't say so. I mean... I get my regular aches and pains, just from getting old, but I haven't been sick. No hospital trips or anything like that lately. My body is holding up pretty well, all things considered.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that someone was putting thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: Putting thoughts into my mind? No... no, I can't say I've ever felt anything like that. My thoughts are just my own. I mean, nobody else is up there putting things in my head, if that's what you're asking. It's just my own regular thinking.\n\nDoctor: Have you been seeing things that other people cannot see?\n\nPatient: Seeing things? No, I can't say I see anything that isn't there. But... well, I do hear things sometimes. Some afternoons, when the house gets real quiet, I hear my old friend. He'll call my name from out on the porch, or sometimes I hear him humming an old choir song. It sounds clear as day, just exactly like he's standing right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you use a hearing aid?\n\nPatient: A hearing aid? No, no I don't. So, no, I don't use anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by a doctor that you have Alzheimer’s disease or any other kind of dementia?\n\nPatient: Alzheimer's? No, no doctor has ever told me that. I mean, my memory ain't perfect—I am 84 years old, after all—but... no, nobody's ever said I have dementia or anything like that. My mind is still doing alright, near as I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble with your vision lately?\n\nPatient: No, my vision is fine.
